LUX SPIRIARESEARCH RESULTS
StudiesAll 36 choicesOverview日本語EN

EXPERIENCE → CONDITIONAL JUDGMENT → RECALL → ACTION

2026-10-30

Can past experience guide the next task?

We set up tasks that actually create JSON, HTML and ZIP files, then compared choices made in 36 fresh AI contexts. We tested whether they could distinguish changed conditions, earlier conditions and conditions they had never encountered.

Both the full loop and the condition without new judgments produced accepted results for all 6 tasks covering the old and changed conditions. With equal results, no additional benefit from adding the new judgment was established.

Connect experience, judgment, paths and action.

Actual tool experience is retained as a conditional LRE judgment, returned through a SHIORI network to a fresh AI context, and used to choose one real operation. New judgments after the change are also reindexed and checked for later return.

There are three independently engineered histories. JSON, HTML and ZIP repeat the same ordering-policy mechanism in different formats; they are not three distinct cognitive abilities. Six teacher, three semantic-reviewer and 36 probe contexts are separate units. Each phase freezes all twelve choices across four arms × three histories before one real operation per item. Probes have no repair or outcome-driven reselection.

Scroll horizontally for every column. The table region is keyboard focusable.

Four comparison arms
ArmAvailable evidence
Full loopHistorical tool evidence, old/new judgments and graph paths
Zero hopsSame history with zero-hop retrieval
No new-judgment writebackRaw experience and old judgments; no new-judgment writeback
No historyNo returned historical evidence

R uses changed conditions, L returns to legacy conditions, and N uses an unobserved opaque profile. Independent audit assesses whether abstention is appropriate in unobserved conditions, separately from exact quotation checks.

An author AI chose the vocabulary and an independent AI reviewed it. Adding a new episode can also change graph degree and scores, so full versus no_writeback compares incremental availability of new judgments. Scores rank retrieval candidates; they are not confidence or probabilities of independent evidence. Automatic capture of all conversation, general Japanese understanding and production integration were not evaluated.

Retain all twelve phase/arm aggregates.

Full loop and no new-judgment writeback had equal acceptance counts in each phase. Returning a new judgment and gaining an additional benefit are separate outcomes. These results do not show additional acceptance gains from a new judgment where raw evidence was sufficient.

Exit zero, artifact acceptance, eligible original-source checks and judgment applicability are distinct. The JSON field all_quotes_exact is a narrow containment check against the 21 disclosed source texts. An abstention with empty evidence is true by the empty-list rule; this does not mean sufficient supporting evidence. Query bytes/characters measure returned packets, not tokens, compute, speed or spending.

New judgments were returned and available, but no probe evidence quoted a native judgment verbatim. Adoption or use of the new judgment was not established. Conditions using raw original-tool evidence had the same acceptance counts; an additional benefit from the new judgment remains unconfirmed.

The eligible original-source check is true for 32/36 rows and false for 4 rows. The false rows quote actual returned unavailability notices. Independent audit confirmed that the notice text matches the actual packet. The check is false because a notice falls outside the preregistered eligible source types of original tool output and native judgment. It does not indicate fabricated quotations or mismatched notice text. The semantic appropriateness of abstention is audited separately.

Rows outside the eligible original-source contract remain retained as false: p-53a4354f367dc1d6, p-39dfcf34f547164a, p-f8f34c879773f1cc, p-188a3d5b8baabb00

What this trial can establish.

Engineered tasks use isolated native components. Full Wake, Emotion, brains, AGI, natural conversation and weight learning were not evaluated. Roles use instruction-based read allowlists on a shared filesystem, not OS isolation.

The three engineered histories repeat one mechanism across formats. They do not generalize to many independent real experiences or everyday conversation. Provider, model, settings, tokens and compute are all unverified and null. Saving, reindexing and recalling a new judgment do not establish weight updates or one subject’s psychological retention.

Hashes identify local record integrity; they are not external signatures or hardware attestation. The public verifier checks disclosed arithmetic, text hashes and quotation containment. It cannot independently prove undisclosed native operations or every claim. Eleven earlier studies and four scroll stories retain their published bytes.
